For each finite set
I
, we define the
semi-local Hecke stack
over
C^{I}
to be
\hk^{\mathrm{sl}}_{G,I}\in\mathrm{Stack}
with
S
-points given by

\hk^{\mathrm{sl}}_{G,I}(S)=\{(c_{I},\mathcal{E}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{1},\phi)|c_{I}\in C^{I}(S),\mathcal{E}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{1}\penalty\ \textup{are $G$-bundles on $D_{c_{I}}$},\phi:\mathcal{E}_{0}|_{\mathring{D}_{c_{I}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{E}_{1}|_{\mathring{D}_{c_{I}}}\}.

We denote the two maps remembering the
G
-bundles by
\overrightarrow{h}_{I,\mathrm{sl}}
and
\overleftarrow{h}_{I,\mathrm{sl}}
.

For each subset
J\subset I
, we define the
J
-th partial (semi-local) Hecke stack
over
C^{I}
to be
\hk^{\mathrm{sl}}_{G,J\subset I}\in\mathrm{Stack}
with
S
-points given by

\hk^{\mathrm{sl}}_{G,J\subset I}(S)=\{(c_{I},\mathcal{E}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{1},\phi)\in\hk^{\mathrm{sl}}_{G,I}|c_{I}\in C^{I}(S),\mathcal{E}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{1}\penalty\ \textup{are $G$-bundles on $D_{c_{I}}$},\phi:\mathcal{E}_{0}|_{D_{c_{I}}\backslash\Gamma_{c_{J}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{E}_{1}|_{D_{c_{I}}\backslash\Gamma_{c_{J}}}\}.

We denote the two maps remembering the
G
-bundles by
\overrightarrow{h}_{J\subset I,\mathrm{sl}}
and
\overleftarrow{h}_{J\subset I,\mathrm{sl}}
. For each element
x\in I
, we denote
\hk^{\mathrm{sl}}_{G,x\in I}:=\hk^{\mathrm{sl}}_{G,\{x\}\subset I}
.

Moreover, given a partition
I=J_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup J_{n}
(which will be denoted by
J_{\bullet}
later), we have the
iterated semi-local Hecke stack

\widetilde{\hk}^{\mathrm{sl}}_{G,J_{\bullet}}\in\mathrm{Stack}
over
C^{I}
defined as

\widetilde{\hk}^{\mathrm{sl}}_{G,J_{\bullet}}:=\hk^{\mathrm{sl}}_{G,J_{1}\subset I}\times_{B(L^{+}G)_{C^{I}}}\cdots\times_{B(L^{+}G)_{C^{I}}}\hk^{\mathrm{sl}}_{G,J_{n}\subset I}.

We denote the two maps remembering the (right-most and left-most)
G
-bundles by
\overrightarrow{h}_{J_{\bullet},\mathrm{sl}}
and
\overleftarrow{h}_{J_{\bullet},\mathrm{sl}}
.

Similar to the local setting, we can define
\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,I},\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,J\subset I},\widetilde{\hk}^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,J_{\bullet}}
by adding a
X
-section over the punctured disc
D_{c_{I}}
to the original moduli problem of the Hecke stack. We also consider the closed substack
\hk^{\mathring{X}_{J\subset I},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,J\subset I}\subset\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,J\subset I}
by requiring that the
X
-section is regular on
D_{c_{I}}\backslash\Gamma_{c_{J}}
throughout the modification.

We also have three versions of global Hecke stacks
\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,I},\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,J\subset I},\widetilde{\hk}^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,J_{\bullet}}
and the version with rational
X
-section
\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,I},\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,J\subset I},\widetilde{\hk}^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,J_{\bullet}},\hk^{\mathring{X}_{J\subset I},\mathrm{g}}_{G,J\subset I}
.

4.3.2.
Sheaves and categories

Consider

\Sat_{G}:=\mathrm{Shv}_{*}(L^{+}G\backslash LG/L^{+}G)

and

\Sat_{G,\hbar}:=\mathrm{Shv}_{*}(\hk^{\mathrm{l}}_{G})

where the cotruncated presentation of
L^{+}G\backslash LG/L^{+}G
is given as usual. These categories are the usual (monoidal) Satake categories without and with loop rotation. We also define
\Sat_{G}^{\mathrm{naive}}:=D\Sat_{G}^{\heartsuit}
(with respect to perverse
t
-structure). Then the classical geometric Satake equivalence proved in
[
26
]
gives an equivalence of symmetric monoidal categories
\Sat_{G}^{\mathrm{naive}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Rep(\check{G})
, and the derived Satake equivalence proved in
[
5
]
gives us equivalence of monoidal categories
\Sat_{G}^{\mathrm{ren}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{QCoh}(\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{*{\mathbin{\mkern-6.0mu\fatslash}}}/\check{G})
8
8

8

Here by
\Sat_{G}^{\mathrm{ren}}
we mean the renormalization such that constructible sheaves become compact.
and
\Sat_{G,\hbar}^{\mathrm{ren}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Mod}^{\check{G}}(U_{\hbar}(\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{{\mathbin{\mkern-6.0mu\fatslash}}}))
, where the shearing
\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{*{\mathbin{\mkern-6.0mu\fatslash}}}=\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}[2]
is defined in
§2.12
in which the
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{gr}}
-action on
\check{\mathfrak{g}}
has weight
-2
. Note that there is a natural equivalence of monoidal categories
D\Sat_{G,\hbar}^{\heartsuit}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}D\Sat_{G}^{\heartsuit}=\Sat_{G}^{\mathrm{naive}}
. This allows us to view
\Rep(\check{G})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Sat_{G}^{\mathrm{naive}}
as full subcategories of both
\Sat_{G}
and
\Sat_{G,\hbar}
. For each
V\in\Rep(\check{G})^{\heartsuit,\omega}
, we write
\IC_{V}
to be the corresponding element in
\Sat_{G}
(or
\Sat_{G,\hbar}
). By our convention
§2.8
, the sheaf
\IC_{V}
has parity congruence to
\langle 2\rho,\lambda_{V}\rangle
where
\lambda_{V}\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
is the highest weight of
V
.

Note that there are natural maps from various Hecke stacks defined in
§4.3.1
to
\hk^{\mathrm{l}}_{G}
(e.g.
\pi:\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{l}}_{G}\to\hk^{\mathrm{l}}_{G}
,
f_{x,\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}^{\hk}:\hk^{\mathrm{sl}}_{G,I}\to\hk^{\mathrm{l}}_{G}
, etc). For each
\mathcal{K}\in\Sat_{G,\hbar}
, we still use
\mathcal{K}
or
\mathcal{K}_{i}
to denote the pull-back of
\mathcal{K}
to the corresponding Hecke stack. In particular, for each
V\in\Rep(\check{G})^{\heartsuit,\omega}
, we get sheaves
\IC_{V}
(
\IC_{V,x}
for
x\in I
) on various Hecke stacks. Moreover, for
V^{I}\in\Rep(\check{G}^{I})^{\heartsuit,\omega}
, we get sheaves
\IC_{V^{I}}
on various Hecke stacks via pull-back and convolution.

Define
\Sat_{X}:=\mathrm{Shv}_{*}(LX/L^{+}G)
9
9

9

The category
\Sat_{X}
is the same as the large category of safe
*
-sheaves
\mathrm{SHV}_{s}^{*}(X_{F}/G_{\mathcal{O}})
defined in
[
4
, §7.2.3, §B.7]
, see
?THM?
.
and
\Sat_{X,\hbar}:=\mathrm{Shv}_{*}(LX/L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))
. The category
\Sat_{X}
is naturally a module over
\Sat_{G}
and the category
\Sat_{X,\hbar}
is natural a module over
\Sat_{G,\hbar}
. Explicitly, the module structure is given by the following: For each
\mathcal{F}\in\Sat_{X,\hbar}
and
\mathcal{K}\in\Sat_{G,\hbar}
we define

\mathcal{K}*\mathcal{F}:=\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\mathrm{l},!}(\overrightarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l}}^{X,*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{K})\in\Sat_{X,\hbar}

where the maps
\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}
,
\overrightarrow{h}^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}
are defined in (
4.22
). Note that for each leg
x\in I
, similar constructions define actions of
\Sat_{G,\hbar}
on

\mathscr{C}=\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{G}\times C^{I}),\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{G,I}^{X}),\mathrm{Shv}((LX/L^{+}G)_{C^{I}}),

and for
\mathcal{F}\in\mathscr{C}
and
\mathcal{K}\in\Sat_{G,\hbar}
, we denote the action at leg
x
by

(4.23)

\mathcal{K}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ _{x}\mathcal{F}\in\mathscr{C}.

For
V^{I}\in\Rep(\check{G})
, we define
T_{V^{I}}:=\overleftarrow{h}_{I,\mathrm{g},!}(\overrightarrow{h}_{I,\mathrm{g}}^{*}(-)\otimes\IC_{V^{I}}):\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{G}\times C^{I})\to\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun_{G}\times C^{I})
. These are the Hecke operators considered in
[
3
]
. By our convention, we have
\IC_{V}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ -\cong T_{c^{*}V}
where
c:\check{G}\to\check{G}
is the Cartan involution.

4.3.3.
Unit object and the Plancherel algebra

The natural map
i_{\mathrm{l}}:L^{+}X/L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D)\to LX/L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D)
defines an object

\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}:=i_{\mathrm{l},*}\underline{k}\in\Sat_{X,\hbar}.

Consider similar natural maps
i_{I,\mathrm{sl}}:(L^{+}X/L^{+}G)_{C^{I}}\to(LX/L^{+}G)_{C^{I}}
and
i_{I,\mathrm{g}}:\Bun_{G}^{X}\times C^{I}\to\Bun^{X}_{G,I}
, we can define

\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{sl}}:=i_{I,\mathrm{sl},*}\underline{k}\in\mathrm{Shv}((LX/L^{+}G)_{C^{I}})

\delta^{X}_{J\subset I,\mathrm{sl}}:=i_{J\subset I,\mathrm{sl},*}\underline{k}\in\mathrm{Shv}((LX/L^{+}G)_{C^{J\subset I}})

and

\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{g}}:=i_{I,\mathrm{g},*}\underline{k}\in\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun^{X}_{G,I})

\delta^{X}_{J\subset I,\mathrm{g}}:=i_{J\subset I,\mathrm{g},*}\underline{k}\in\mathrm{Shv}(\Bun^{X}_{G,J\subset I}).

Regarding
\Sat_{X,\hbar}\in\mathrm{Mod}(\Sat_{G,\hbar})
, from the object
\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}
we can construct its inner endomorphism algebra object

(4.24)

\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}:=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\Sat_{G,\hbar}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}})\in\Sat_{G,\hbar}

which is characterized by the property that for each object
\mathcal{K}\in\Sat_{G,\hbar}
there exists a functorial isomorphism

\Hom_{\Sat_{G,\hbar}}(\mathcal{K},\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\Sat_{X,\hbar}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}))=\Hom_{\Sat_{G,\hbar}}(\mathcal{K}*\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}).

This is the (non-commutative) Plancherel algebra introduced in
[
4
, §8.5]
. Similarly, we can regard
\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\in\Sat_{X}\in\mathrm{Mod}(\Sat_{G})
and define the (commutative) Plancherel algebra

\mathrm{PL}_{X}:=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\Sat_{G}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}})\in\Sat_{G}.

By replacing the monoidal categories
\Sat_{G,\hbar}
and
\Sat_{G}
by their common monoidal subcategory
\Sat_{G}^{\mathrm{naive}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Rep(\check{G})
, we can regard both Plancherel algebras as objects
\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar},\mathrm{PL}_{X}\in\Rep(\check{G})
by applying the right adjoint of the natural inclusion
\Sat_{G}^{\mathrm{naive}}\to\Sat_{G,\hbar}
(and
\Sat_{G}^{\mathrm{naive}}\to\Sat_{G}
). Note that
\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}
is naturally equipped with a map from the algebra (with trivial
\check{G}
-action)
\Gamma(B\Aut(D),\underline{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Gamma(B\mathbb{G}_{m},\underline{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}k[\hbar]
where the last isomorphism is normalized in
§2.11
.

The commutative and non-commutative Plancherel algebras are related by
\mathrm{PL}_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}\otimes_{k[\hbar]}k.
We have the following conjecture from
[
4
]
:

Conjecture 4.36
.

[
4
, Conjecture
8.1.8&8.5.2]

Suppose the
G
-Hamiltonian space
T^{*}X
is hyperspherical, then the following properties hold:

(1)

The non-commutative Plancherel algebra
\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}
is flat over
k[\hbar]
;

(2)

The Plancherel algebra without loop rotation
\mathrm{PL}_{X}
is commutative;

(3)

There is an isomorphism of
\check{G}
-equivariant commutative 2-shifted Poisson algebras
\mathrm{PL}_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{O}(\check{M}^{{\mathbin{\mkern-6.0mu\fatslash}}})
. Moreover, there is a canonical isomorphism
\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{O}_{\hbar}(\check{M}^{{\mathbin{\mkern-6.0mu\fatslash}}})
between quantization of both Poisson algebras. Here
\check{M}
is the hyperspherical dual of
T^{*}X
introduced in
§2.7
, and the shearing
\check{M}^{{\mathbin{\mkern-6.0mu\fatslash}}}
is defined in
§2.12
.
10
10

10

When
X
is not homogeneous, one needs to twist the
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{gr}}
-action on
\check{M}
or the
\Sat_{G,\hbar}
-module structure on
\Sat_{X,\hbar}
. We refer to
[
4
, §8]
for details.

Remark 4.37
.

We only state part of the conjectures made in
[
4
]
, which is relevant to our article.

4.3.4.
Period sheaf

Consider the map
\pi:\Bun_{G}^{X}\to\Bun_{G}
, we define the
X
-period sheaf to be

\mathcal{P}_{X}:=\pi_{!}\underline{k}_{\Bun_{G}^{X}}.

Remark 4.38
.

As we have mentioned in
?THM?
, when we consider hyperspherical
T^{*}X
(see
§2.6
for a setup), if the
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{gr}}
-action on
X
is trivial (equivalently speaking: when
X
is
G
-homogeneous), the object
\mathcal{P}_{X}
is the unnormalized
!
-period sheaf introduced in
[
4
, §10.3]
. When the
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{gr}}
-action on
X
is non-trivial, this is different from the unnormalized period sheaf in
loc.cit
by a half-spin twist. One can easily adapt these normalizations when needed.

4.3.5.
Summary of notations

The notations in this section are complicated, and we will use them throughout the article. We summarize the rules that we use to design our notations here for the convenience of the reader.

We use the word
local
to indicate moduli of something over the disc
D
(or punctured disc
\mathring{D}
). We use
semi-local
to indicate moduli of something over the disc
D_{c}
(or punctured disc
\mathring{D}_{c}
) for a moving point
c\in C
(or several points
c\in C^{I}
). We use the word
global
to indicate moduli of something over the curve (or punctured curve, possibly several copies of the curve). Whenever we write a moduli (or map between moduli) that is local/semi-local/global, we would add a subscript (or sometimes superscript)
\mathrm{l},\mathrm{sl},\mathrm{g}
to indicate this whenever it is unclear from other notations. We sometimes use the subscript
\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}
to indicate a morphism is mapping from semi-local moduli to local moduli, or global moduli to semi-local moduli.

We always use
\hk
to indicate Hecke-type stacks (i.e., do modification one time, but can happen at several legs) and
\widetilde{\hk}
to indicate iterated Hecke-type stacks (i.e, do modification several times). We use
\overleftarrow{h}
and
\overrightarrow{h}
to denote the map sending a Hecke modification to its left-most object and right-most object, respectively. We use the word
leg
to describe the point at which a Hecke modification happens or an
X
-section is not required to be regular. We use a superscript
\hk
(or
\widetilde{\hk}
) on a morphism to indicate that the morphism is between Hecke-type stacks.

We use a superscript
X
to indicate a moduli of objects equipped with an
X
-section (the
X
-section may or may not be regular, depending on the context). When we use this for Hecke-type stacks, we sometimes write
\mathring{X}
to emphasize that the
X
-section is not required to be regular (This is to avoid confusion with the relative Grassmanian
\Gr^{X}_{G}
in which the
X
-section is regular on both sides of the modification which will be introduced in
§5.1
).

We use
I
to denote the index set of legs. We use the letter
x
to indicate an element
x\in I
. When we have subscript
J\subset I
or
x\in I
, we mean the Hecke modification happens only at
J
(or i.e.
\{x\}
) or the
X
-section is rational at
J
(or
\{x\}
).

We always use
l
to denote a map remembering all the legs. We use
f
to denote maps between moduli of different levels (i.e., local/semi-local/global). We use
i
to denote maps from moduli with regular
X
-section to moduli with meromorphic
X
-section. We use
r_{x}
to denote a morphism remembering only information at the leg
x
. We use
\pi
to denote maps forgetting the
X
-section. We use
w
to denote the maps forgetting the legs. We use
\Delta
to denote diagonal-type maps.

When we write
\IC_{V}
,
\IC_{V^{I}}
, we may mean sheaves on any Hecke-type stack (which would be clear from the context) to which the intersection complexes in
\Sat_{G}
can be pulled
back as kernel sheaves for some cohomological correspondence. We sometimes write
\IC_{V,x}
to indicate that the sheaf is pulled back from leg
x
.

We use
*
to denote the convolution in the local categories, and
\square
\star
to denote the action of objects in the local categories on global/semi-local categories (or the action of kernel sheaves on some Hecke-type moduli by pull-tensor-push). We sometimes use
\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ _{x}
to indicate the action happens at leg
x
.

4.4.
Construction of special cohomological correspondence

In this section, we introduce the local-to-global construction of special cohomological correspondences. The main output of this section is
?THM?
.

For notations on the moduli spaces and morphisms between them, we refer to
§4.3.1
(see
§4.3.5
for a summary).

Definition 4.39
.

A
local special cohomological correspondence of type
V\in\Rep(\check{G})^{\heartsuit,\omega}
of degree
d
is an element

c_{V}^{\mathrm{l}}\in\Hom_{\check{G}}(V\langle-d\rangle,\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}).

Equivalently, consider the diagram (
4.22
), we can regard
c_{V}^{\mathrm{l}}
as a cohomological correspondence by the tautological identification

\begin{split}\Hom_{\check{G}}(V\langle-d\rangle,\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar})&=\Hom_{\Sat_{X,\hbar}}(\IC_{V}*\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle d\rangle)\\
&=\Hom(\overleftarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l},!}^{X}(\overrightarrow{h}_{\mathrm{l}}^{X,*}\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\otimes\IC_{V}),\delta_{l}^{X}\langle d\rangle)\\
&=\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{l}}_{G},\IC_{V}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle d\rangle)\end{split}.

Now we apply the pull-back functoriality in
§4.2.3
to produce special cohomological correspondences at the semi-local and global levels. Consider the following diagram:

(4.25)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun^{X}_{G,\{1\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun^{X}_{G,\{1\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(LX/L^{+}G)_{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,\{1\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(LX/L^{+}G)_{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces LX/L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{l}}_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces LX/L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\hk}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\hk}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{h}^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

Note that both left squares are Cartesian (in fact, all squares are Cartesian), from the isomorphisms
f_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}^{*}\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{sl}}
and
f_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}^{*}\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{sl}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{g}}
, we get cohomological correspondences

(4.26)

c_{V}^{\mathrm{sl}}:=f_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}^{*}c_{V}^{\mathrm{l}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,\{1\}},\IC_{V}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{sl}}\langle d\rangle)

and

(4.27)

c_{V}^{\mathrm{g}}:=f_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}^{*}c_{V}^{\mathrm{sl}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1\}},\IC_{V}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{g}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{g}}\langle d\rangle).

Now we construct cohomological correspondences with legs indexed by a finite set
I
. To begin with, for any
x\in I
, consider the following diagram

(4.28)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(LX/L^{+}G)_{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,\{1\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(LX/L^{+}G)_{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(LX/L^{+}G)_{C^{x\in I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hk^{\mathring{X}_{x\in I},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,x\in I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(LX/L^{+}G)_{C^{x\in I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(LX/L^{+}G)_{C^{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,x\in I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(LX/L^{+}G)_{C^{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\urcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{x,\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{x\in I,\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overleftarrow{h}^{X_{x\in I}}_{x\in I,\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{h}^{X_{x\in I}}_{x\in I,\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{x,\mathrm{sl}}^{\hk}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{x\in I,\mathrm{sl}}^{\hk}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{x,\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{x\in I,\mathrm{sl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{x\in I,\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{h}^{X}_{x\in I,\mathrm{sl}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

Note that all the squares above are Cartesian, and we have natural isomorphism
r_{x,\mathrm{sl}}^{*}\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{sl}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\delta^{X}_{x\in I,\mathrm{sl}}
and
\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{sl}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}i_{x\in I,\mathrm{sl},*}\delta^{X}_{x\in I,\mathrm{sl}}
. By applying the pull-back functoriality for the upper diagram and push-forward functoriality for the lower diagram, we get cohomological correspondences

(4.29)

c_{V,x\in I}^{\mathrm{sl}}:=r_{x,\mathrm{sl}}^{*}c_{V}^{\mathrm{sl}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X}_{x\in I},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,x\in I},\IC_{V,x}}(\delta^{X}_{x\in I,\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{x\in I,\mathrm{sl}}\langle d\rangle)

and

(4.30)

c_{V,x}^{\mathrm{sl}}:=i_{x\in I,\mathrm{sl},!}c_{V,x\in I}^{\mathrm{sl}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,x\in I},\IC_{V,x}}(\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{sl}}\langle d\rangle).

We also have a global counterpart of the picture above: Consider the diagram

From the natural isomorphism
r_{x,\mathrm{g}}^{*}\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{g}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\delta^{X}_{x\in I,\mathrm{g}}
and
\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{g}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}i_{x\in I,\mathrm{g},*}\delta^{X}_{x\in I,\mathrm{g}}
, we get cohomological correspondences

(4.32)

c_{V,x\in I}^{\mathrm{g}}:=r_{x,\mathrm{g}}^{*}c_{V}^{\mathrm{g}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X}_{x\in I},\mathrm{g}}_{G,x\in I},\IC_{V,x}}(\delta^{X}_{x\in I,\mathrm{g}},\delta^{X}_{x\in I,\mathrm{g}}\langle d\rangle)

and

(4.33)

c_{V,x}^{\mathrm{g}}:=i_{x\in I,\mathrm{g},!}c_{V,x\in I}^{\mathrm{g}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,x\in I},\IC_{V,x}}(\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{g}},\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{g}}\langle d\rangle).

From now on, we drop the subscript
J_{\bullet}
in the notation for iterated Hecke stacks and always use the finest partition. We are going to construct global special cohomological correspondences and variants out of local special cohomological correspondences.

Definition 4.40
.

We define a
local special cohomological correspondences datum
to be a tuple

(I,V^{I},\{c_{V^{x}}^{\mathrm{l}}\}_{x\in I})

where

•

I
is a finite set;

•

V^{\boxtimes I}\in\Rep(\check{G}^{I})^{\heartsuit,\omega}
;

•

For each
x\in I
,
c_{V^{x}}^{\mathrm{l}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{l}}_{G},\IC_{V^{x}}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle d_{x}\rangle)
is a local special cohomological correspondence of degree
d_{x}
as in
?THM?
.

Set
d_{I}=\sum_{x\in I}d_{x}
to be the degree of
(I,V^{I},\{c_{V^{x}}^{\mathrm{l}}\}_{x\in I})
. Suppose
I=[r]
.

(1)

We define the
local composition
of
(I,V^{I},\{c_{V^{x}}^{\mathrm{l}}\}_{x\in I})
to be the element

(4.34)

c^{\mathrm{l}}_{V^{I}}:=c^{\mathrm{l}}_{V^{1}}\circ\cdots\circ c^{\mathrm{l}}_{V^{r}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{l}}_{G},\IC_{V^{\otimes I}}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle d_{I}\rangle),

(2)

We define the
semi-local special cohomological correspondence
of
(I,V^{I},\{c_{V^{x}}^{\mathrm{l}}\}_{x\in I})
to be the element

(4.35)

c^{\mathrm{sl}}_{V^{I},I}:=c^{\mathrm{sl}}_{V^{1},1}\circ\cdots\circ c^{\mathrm{sl}}_{V^{r},r}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\widetilde{\hk}^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{sl}}\langle d_{I}\rangle)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{sl}}\langle d_{I}\rangle);

where the elements
c^{\mathrm{sl}}_{V^{x},x}
are defined in (
4.30
);

(3)

We define the
global special cohomological correspondence
of
(I,V^{I},\{c_{V^{x}}^{\mathrm{l}}\}_{x\in I})
to be the element

(4.36)

c^{\mathrm{g}}_{V^{I},I}:=c^{\mathrm{g}}_{V^{1},1}\circ\cdots\circ c^{\mathrm{g}}_{V^{r},r}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\widetilde{\hk}^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{g}},\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{g}}\langle d_{I}\rangle)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{g}},\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{g}}\langle d_{I}\rangle),

where the elements
c^{\mathrm{g}}_{V^{x},x}
are defined in (
4.33
).

We are finally able to define special cohomological correspondences for the period sheaf
\mathcal{P}_{X}
.

Definition 4.41
.

Continuing the setting of
?THM?
, consider the diagram

(4.37)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun^{X}_{G,I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun^{X}_{G,I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{G}\times C^{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,I}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overleftarrow{h}_{I,\mathrm{g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{h}_{I,\mathrm{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

where both squares are Cartesian. Note that we have natural isomorphism
\pi_{I,!}\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{g}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}}
.

(1)

We define the
extended special cohomological correspondence
associated to
(I,V^{I},\{c_{V^{x}}^{\mathrm{l}}\}_{x\in I})
to be the element

(4.38)

c_{V^{I},I}:=\pi_{I,!}c^{\mathrm{g}}_{V^{I},I}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}}\langle d_{I}\rangle)

where the element
c^{\mathrm{g}}_{V^{I},I}
is defined in (
4.36
);

(2)

Consider the map
w_{I}:\Bun_{G}^{X}\times C^{I}\to\Bun_{G}^{X}
. We define the
special cohomological correspondence
associated to
(I,V^{I},\{c_{V^{x}}^{\mathrm{l}}\}_{x\in I})
to be the element

(4.39)

c_{V^{I}}:=[C^{I}]\circ c_{V^{I},I}\circ\taut_{C^{I}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\mathcal{P}_{X},\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle d_{I}-2|I|\rangle)

where the cohomological correspondences
\taut_{C^{I}},[C^{I}]
are introduced in

,

4.5.
Main result

In this section, we state the so-called automorphic commutator relation
?THM?
(partially proved in
?THM?
). This is the main result in
§4
.

4.5.1.
Local datum

We now introduce the local special cohomological correspondence datum needed to formulate the commutator relation.

Setting 4.42
.

We fix a local special cohomological correspondence datum

(I,V^{I},\{c_{V^{x}}^{\mathrm{l}}\}_{x\in I})

as in
?THM?
, in which we take
I=[r]
.

We fix
x\in[r-1]\subset I
. Consider
I^{\prime}:=I-\{x,x+1\}
and
[I]:=[r-1]
. Denote
V=V_{x}
and
W=V_{x+1}
. Set
d_{V}=d_{x}
and
d_{W}=d_{x+1}
.

Consider
\mathrm{sw}_{x,x+1}\in\Aut(I)
to be the element swapping
x
and
x+1
, and
\Delta_{x,x+1}\in\Hom(I,[I])
to be the order-preserving map collapsing
x
and
x+1
. These maps induce natural maps
\mathrm{sw}_{G,x,x+1}\in\Aut(BG^{I})
,
\Delta_{G,x,x+1}\in\Hom(BG^{[I]},BG^{I})
. We denote
V^{I,\mathrm{sw}}:=\mathrm{sw}_{G,x,x+1}^{*}V^{I}\in\Rep(\check{G}^{I})^{\heartsuit,\omega}
and
V^{[I]}:=\Delta_{G,x,x+1}^{*}V^{I}\in\Rep(\check{G}^{[I]})^{\heartsuit,\omega}
.

Assume
?THM?
(see below), we consider two more local special cohomological correspondence data

(I,V^{I,\mathrm{sw}},\{c_{V^{x}}^{\mathrm{l}}\}_{x\in I})

and

([I],V^{[I]},\{c_{V^{x}}^{\mathrm{l}}\}_{x\in[I]})

where the local special cohomological correspondences remain to be the old ones except we choose

(4.40)

c^{\mathrm{l}}_{V^{x}\otimes V^{x+1}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk_{G}^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{l}},\IC_{V^{x}\otimes V^{x+1}}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle d_{x}+d_{x+1}-2\rangle).

to be the element in
?THM?
(
2
)
.

From these local cohomological correspondences, we can run the construction in
§4.4
and get

(4.41)

c_{V^{I}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\mathcal{P}_{X},\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle d_{I}-2|I|\rangle),

(4.42)

c_{V^{I,\mathrm{sw}}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I,\mathrm{sw}}}}(\mathcal{P}_{X},\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle d_{I}-2|I|\rangle),

and

(4.43)

c_{V^{[I]}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,[I]},\IC_{V^{[I]}}}(\mathcal{P}_{X},\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle d_{I}-2|I|\rangle)

defined in (
4.39
). Here we are using
d_{[I]}-2|[I]|=(d_{I}-2)-2(|I|-1)=d_{I}-2|I|
in the last formula.

Note that we have an isomorphism
\Delta^{\hk^{*}}_{x,x+1}\IC_{V^{I}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\IC_{V^{[I]}}
. The push-forward functoriality in
?THM?
gives us

(4.45)

\Delta^{\hk}_{x,x+1,!}c_{V^{[I]}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\mathcal{P}_{X},\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle d_{I}-2|I|\rangle).

4.5.3.
Commutator in the Plancherel algebra

The natural multiplication map
m:\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}\otimes\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}\to\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}
on the (non-commutative) Plancherel algebra can be characterized as following: Given
V,W\in\Rep(\check{G})^{\heartsuit,\omega}
and

c_{V}^{\mathrm{l}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{l}}_{G},\IC_{V}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle d_{V}\rangle),

c_{W}^{\mathrm{l}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{l}}_{G},\IC_{W}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle d_{W}\rangle),

the product
V\otimes W\langle-d_{V}-d_{W}\rangle\to\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}\otimes\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}\to\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}
is defined by

c_{V\boxtimes W}^{\mathrm{l}}=c_{V}^{\mathrm{l}}\circ c_{W}^{\mathrm{l}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{l}}_{G},\IC_{V\otimes W}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle).

Similarly, we have

c^{\mathrm{l}}_{W\boxtimes V}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{l}}_{G},\IC_{W\otimes V}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle).

Using the natural isomorphism

\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{l}}_{G},\IC_{W\otimes V}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{l}}_{G},\IC_{V\otimes W}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle),

we can view both
c^{\mathrm{l}}_{V\boxtimes W}
and
c^{\mathrm{l}}_{W\boxtimes V}
as elements in the same vector space
\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{l}}_{G},\IC_{V\otimes W}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle).
We denote

[c_{V}^{\mathrm{l}},c_{W}^{\mathrm{l}}]:=c^{\mathrm{l}}_{V\boxtimes W}-c^{\mathrm{l}}_{W\boxtimes V}.

We make the following assumption:

Assumption 4.43
.

The following two things hold:

(1)

The multiplication-by-
\hbar
map

\cdot\hbar:\Hom^{0}(V\otimes W\langle-d_{V}-d_{W}+2\rangle,\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar})\to\Hom^{0}(V\otimes W\langle-d_{V}-d_{W}\rangle,\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar})

is injective;

(2)

There exists an element
c_{V\otimes W}^{\mathrm{l}}\in\Hom^{0}(V\otimes W\langle-d_{V}-d_{W}+2\rangle,\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar})
such that

c_{V\otimes W}^{\mathrm{l}}\cdot\hbar=[c_{V}^{\mathrm{l}},c_{W}^{\mathrm{l}}].

Remark 4.44
.

Note that in the case
T^{*}X
is a hyperspherical
G
-Hamiltonian space,
?THM?
above is a direct consequence of
?THM?
.

4.5.4.
Automorphic commutator relations

Assume we are in
?THM?
, we get three special cohomological correspondences

c_{V^{I}},c_{V^{I,\mathrm{sw}}},\Delta^{\hk}_{x,x+1,!}c_{V^{[I]}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\mathcal{P}_{X},\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle d_{I}-2|I|\rangle)

where the last one is defined in (
4.45
). Our main conjecture in this section is the following:

Conjecture 4.45

(Automorphic commutator relation)
.

Under
?THM?
, we have the following identity:

c_{V^{I}}-c_{V^{I,\mathrm{sw}}}=\Delta^{\hk}_{x,x+1,!}c_{V^{[I]}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\mathcal{P}_{X},\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle d_{I}-2|I|\rangle).

We are currently not able to prove
?THM?
in general but are only able to prove it under some additional technical assumptions. The additional condition we have to add is the following:

Assumption 4.46
.

The following hold:

(1)

For
d=1,2,3
, we have
\Hom^{0}(V\otimes W\langle-d_{V}-d_{W}+2\rangle[d],\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar})=0

(2)

Let
g=g(C)
to be the genus of the curve
C
. We have
g\neq 1
.

Our main theorem in this section is the following, which will be proved in
§4.6
:

Theorem 4.47
.

Assume
?THM?
, then
?THM?
is true.

Remark 4.48
.

We are mostly interested in the middle-dimensional case (i.e.
d_{V}+d_{W}=2
, see
?THM?
) and
T^{*}X
is tempered hyperspherical as defined in
§2.7
. In this case,
?THM?
(
1
)

just follows from
?THM?
(
3
)
which implies that
\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}
is concentrated in nonnegative cohomological degree.

Therefore,
?THM?
(
1
)
conjecturally holds in the middle-dimensional tempered case.
?THM?
(
2
)
is more restrictive, and we would like to remove it in the future. We believe that all the additional assumptions in
?THM?
can be removed at the same time once a more essential argument is discovered.

Remark 4.49
.

One can also formulate and prove
?THM?
without assuming
?THM?
when taking
C=[\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
. The key point making this case provable is that
?THM?
is automatically true in this case. This is the reason that we believe
?THM?
is true even without
?THM?
. We would hope one can prove
?THM?
by reducing to the case
C=[\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
, but we do not know how to achieve this.

Remark 4.50
.

Instead of using the Plancherel algebra with loop rotation, it may be more conceptual to formulate
?THM?
using the
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-Plancherel algebra and factorization structure developed in
[
4
, §16]
. However, as far as we know, the factorizable local conjecture is far from being known or even well-formulated, while the loop-rotated version seems to be reachable or known in many cases. Therefore, we choose to use the loop-rotated version rather than the factorizable version.

4.6.
Proof of global commutator relations

In this section, we give the proof of
?THM?
. The idea is easy: One first reduces to the case
|I|=2
, which is done in
§4.6.1
. After that, one reduces to a semi-local statement (
?THM?
), which is the main subject of
§4.6.2
and
§4.6.3
. Finally, one relates the semi-local statement to the local commutator relation
?THM?
(
2
)
and concludes the proof in
§4.6.4
.

4.6.1.
Reduction to the case
|I|=2

To reduce the proof of
?THM?
to the case
|I|=2
, by compatibility of push-forward functoriality in
?THM?
and composition in
§4.2.4
, we only need to prove the following:

Proposition 4.51
.

Under
?THM?
, we have

c_{V^{I}}=c_{V^{1}}\circ c_{V^{2}}\circ\cdots\circ c_{V^{r}}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\widetilde{\hk}^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\mathcal{P}_{X},\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle d_{I}-2|I|\rangle)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\mathcal{P}_{X},\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle d_{I}-2|I|\rangle)

where
c_{V^{I}}
and
c_{V^{x}}
for
x\in I
are both defined via (
4.39
).

Proof of
?THM?
.

We use
C_{1},\cdots,C_{r}
to distinguish the different factors of
C^{I}
.
By (
4.36
), (
4.38
), (
4.39
), and compatibility of push-forward and composition (and compatibility between pull-back and composition), we know

c_{V^{I}}=[C^{I}]\circ c_{V^{1},1}\circ c_{V^{2},2}\circ\cdots\circ c_{V^{r},r}\circ\taut_{C^{I}}.

On the other hand, we have

c_{V^{1}}\circ c_{V^{2}}\circ\cdots\circ c_{V^{r}}=[C_{1}]\circ c_{V^{1},1}\circ\taut_{C_{1}}\circ\cdots\circ[C_{r}]\circ c_{V^{r},r}\circ\taut_{C_{r}}.

Then it is easy to check that one can exchange two correspondences appearing in the formula above whenever they have different subindices
x\in I
.
∎

4.6.2.
A reformulation of
?THM?

From now on, we assume
I=\{1,2\}
and
[I]=\{1\}
.

?THM?
is formulated in terms of special cohomological correspondences (
4.39
). In this section, we reformulate
?THM?
using (
4.38
).

We have a map

(4.47)

\begin{split}\mathrm{coev}:&\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1\}},\IC_{V\otimes W}}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}-2\rangle)\\
\to&\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1,2\}},\IC_{V\boxtimes W}}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)\end{split}

defined by

(4.48)

\begin{split}\mathrm{coev}:&\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1\}},\IC_{V\otimes W}}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}-2\rangle)\\
=&\Hom(\IC_{V\otimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ (\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C}),\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}-2\rangle)\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}&\Hom((\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})^{*}\IC_{V\boxtimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ (\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}}),(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})^{*}\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}-2\rangle)\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}&\Hom((\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})^{*}\IC_{V\boxtimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ (\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}}),(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})^{!}\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}&\Hom(\IC_{V\boxtimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ (\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}}),(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})^{!}\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)\\
\to&\Hom(\IC_{V\boxtimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ (\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}}),\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)\\
=&\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1,2\}},\IC_{V\boxtimes W}}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)\end{split}

Where the third step uses the purity isomorphism
?THM?
.

We can reformulate
?THM?
as follows:

Conjecture 4.52
.

Under
?THM?
, we have the following identity:

c_{V\boxtimes W,\{1,2\}}-c_{W\boxtimes V,\{1,2\}}=\mathrm{coev}(c_{V\otimes W,\{1\}})\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1,2\}},\IC_{V\boxtimes W}}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)

where the elements
c_{V\boxtimes W,\{1,2\}},c_{W\boxtimes V,\{1,2\}},c_{V\otimes W,\{1\}}
are both defined via (
4.38
).

Proof of
?THM?
assuming
?THM?
.

Recall the map forgetting legs
w_{I}:\Bun_{G}\times C^{I}\to\Bun_{G}
. Consider the map

\begin{split}\phi_{\{1,2\}}:=[C^{2}]\circ(\bullet)\circ\taut_{C^{2}}:&\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1,2\}},\IC_{V\boxtimes W}}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)\\
\to&\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1,2\}},\IC_{V\boxtimes W}}(\mathcal{P}_{X},\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}-4\rangle)\end{split}

It follows from the definition in (
4.39
) that

\phi_{\{1,2\}}(c_{V\boxtimes W,\{1,2\}}-c_{W\boxtimes V,\{1,2\}})=c_{V\boxtimes W}-c_{W\boxtimes V}.

Therefore, it suffices to prove the following two maps coincide:

(4.49)

\begin{split}\phi_{\{1,2\}}\circ\mathrm{coev}=\Delta^{\hk}_{\mathrm{g},!}\circ\phi_{\{1\}}:&\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1\}},\IC_{V\otimes W}}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}-2\rangle)\\
\to&\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1,2\}},\IC_{V\boxtimes W}}(\mathcal{P}_{X},\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}-4\rangle)\end{split}

where
\phi_{\{1\}}=[C]\circ(\bullet)\circ\taut_{C}
is the one-leg analogue of
\phi_{\{1,2\}}
.
Consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{G}\times C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{G}\times C^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1,2\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{G}\times C^{2},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{\hk}_{\mathrm{g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overleftarrow{h}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{h}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overleftarrow{h}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}}

where the square is Cartesian.
Denote

\mathcal{F}:=\overrightarrow{h}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}^{*}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}})\otimes\IC_{V\boxtimes W}\langle-d_{V}-d_{W}+2\rangle\in\mathrm{Shv}(\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1,2\}}).

We can identify both maps in (
4.49
) as maps

(4.50)

\phi_{\{1,2\}}\circ\mathrm{coev}=\Delta^{\hk}_{!}\circ\phi_{\{1\}}:\Hom(\overleftarrow{h}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g},!}\Delta^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{g}}\mathcal{F},w_{\{1\}}^{!}\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle-2\rangle)\to\Hom(w_{\{1,2\},!}\overleftarrow{h}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g},!}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle-2\rangle)

For
\alpha\in\Hom(\overleftarrow{h}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g},!}\Delta^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{g}}\mathcal{F},w_{\{1\}}^{*}\mathcal{P}_{X})
, unwinding the definition of all the maps given in (
4.45
) and (
4.48
), we have the following diagrams:

(4.51)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{\{1,2\},!}\overleftarrow{h}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g},!}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{\{1,2\},!}\overleftarrow{h}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g},!}\Delta^{\hk}_{\mathrm{g},!}\Delta^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{g}}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{\{1,2\},!}(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}\overleftarrow{h}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g},!}\Delta^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{g}}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{\{1,2\},!}\overleftarrow{h}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g},!}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{\{1,2\},!}(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})^{*}\overleftarrow{h}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g},!}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{\{1,2\},!}(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}\overleftarrow{h}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g},!}\Delta^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{g}}\mathcal{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

(4.52)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{\{1,2\},!}(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}w_{\{1\}}^{!}\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle-2\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{\{1\},!}w_{\{1\}}^{!}\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle-2\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle-2\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{\{1,2\},!}(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}w_{\{1\}}^{!}\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle-2\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{\{1,2\},!}(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})^{!}w_{\{1,2\}}^{!}\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle-2\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}_{X}\langle-2\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

Note that
\Delta^{\hk}_{!}\circ\phi_{\{1\}}(\alpha)
is given by the composition of the upper row of (
4.51
) and
w_{\{1,2\},!}(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}\alpha
and the upper row of (
4.52
), and
\phi_{\{1,2\}}\circ\mathrm{coev}(\alpha)
is given by the composition of the lower row of (
4.51
) and
w_{\{1,2\},!}(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}\alpha
and the lower row of (
4.52
).
The commutativity of the diagram (
4.51
) follows from
?THM?
. The commutativity of the diagram (
4.52
) follows from a similar argument. This concludes the proof.
∎

4.6.3.
Global and semi-local variants of
?THM?

In this section, we formulate variants of
?THM?
in terms of (
4.36
) and (
4.35
) and reduce the proof of the original statement to these variants.

Consider the diagram parallel to (
4.46
):

(4.53)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun_{G,\{1\}}^{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1\}}}

and also the semi-local version

(4.54)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(LX/L^{+}G)_{C}}

We have maps of vector spaces similar to (
4.47
):

(4.55)

\begin{split}\mathrm{coev}_{\mathrm{g}}:&\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1\}},\IC_{V\otimes W}}(\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}},\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}-2\rangle)\\
\to&\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1,2\}},\IC_{V\boxtimes W}}(\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}},\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)\end{split}

and

(4.56)

\begin{split}\mathrm{coev}_{\mathrm{sl}}:&\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,\{1\}},\IC_{V\otimes W}}(\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}-2\rangle)\\
\to&\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,\{1,2\}},\IC_{V\boxtimes W}}(\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)\end{split}

whose definition is completely parallel to (
4.48
) given the following lemma:

Lemma 4.53
.

We have
\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Delta^{X,!}_{\mathrm{g}}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}\langle 2\rangle
and
\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Delta^{X,!}_{\mathrm{sl}}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}\langle 2\rangle
.

Proof of lemma.

Consider

(4.57)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bun^{X}_{G}\times C}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

and

(4.58)

\hbox to196.26pt{\vbox to63.27pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 98.13011pt\lower-26.92952pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

By proper base change, we only need to show the purity transformations

(4.59)

[\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C}]:\underline{k}_{\Bun_{G}^{X}\times C}\to(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})^{!}\underline{k}_{\Bun_{G}^{X}\times C^{2}}

and

(4.60)

[\Delta^{X,+}_{\mathrm{sl}}]:\underline{k}_{(L^{+}X/L^{+}G)_{C}}\to\Delta^{X,+,!}_{\mathrm{sl}}\underline{k}_{(L^{+}X/L^{+}G)_{C^{2}}}

are isomorphisms. Here, both fundamental classes are defined via
?THM?
, where the second one uses the Cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(L^{+}X/L^{+}G)_{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(L^{+}X/L^{+}G)_{C^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{X,+}_{\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

which makes the map
{\Delta^{X,+}_{\mathrm{sl}}}
quasi-smooth as in
?THM?
.

The first isomorphism follows from
?THM?
and the second follows from
?THM?
and
?THM?
(
1
)
(note that

,
(L^{+}X)_{C^{2}}\rightarrow C^{2}
are pro-smooth surjection, and

is qcqs).

∎

We can now formulate global and semi-local variants (or primitive versions) of
?THM?
:

Conjecture 4.54

(Global version)
.

Under
?THM?
, we have the following identity:

c_{V\boxtimes W,\{1,2\}}^{\mathrm{g}}-c_{W\boxtimes V,\{1,2\}}^{\mathrm{g}}=\mathrm{coev}_{\mathrm{g}}(c_{V\otimes W,\{1\}}^{\mathrm{g}})\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1,2\}},\IC_{V\boxtimes W}}(\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}},\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)

where the elements
c_{V\boxtimes W,\{1,2\}}^{\mathrm{g}},c_{W\boxtimes V,\{1,2\}}^{\mathrm{g}},c_{V\otimes W,\{1\}}^{\mathrm{g}}
are both defined via (
4.36
).

Conjecture 4.55

(Semi-local version)
.

Under
?THM?
, we have the following identity:

c_{V\boxtimes W,\{1,2\}}^{\mathrm{sl}}-c_{W\boxtimes V,\{1,2\}}^{\mathrm{sl}}=\mathrm{coev}_{\mathrm{sl}}(c_{V\otimes W,\{1\}}^{\mathrm{sl}})\in\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,\{1,2\}},\IC_{V\boxtimes W}}(\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)

where the elements
c_{V\boxtimes W,\{1,2\}}^{\mathrm{sl}},c_{W\boxtimes V,\{1,2\}}^{\mathrm{sl}},c_{V\otimes W,\{1\}}^{\mathrm{sl}}
are both defined via (
4.35
).

?THM?
from

and then deduce
?THM?
from
?THM?
.

Proof of
?THM?
assuming
?THM?
.

Take
I=\{1\},\{1,2\}
in the diagram (
4.37
). The push-forward functoriality in
§4.2.3
gives us

(4.61)

\pi_{I,!}:\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{g}},\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{g}}\langle d_{I}\rangle)\to\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathrm{g}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}},\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{I}}\langle d_{I}\rangle)

for
I=\{1\},\{1,2\}
. We only need to show

(4.62)

\begin{split}\pi_{\{1,2\},!}\circ\mathrm{coev}_{\mathrm{g}}=\mathrm{coev}\circ\pi_{\{1\},!}:&\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1\}},\IC_{V\otimes W}}(\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}},\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}-2\rangle)\\
\to&\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,\{1,2\}},\IC_{V\boxtimes W}}(\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)\end{split}.

The proof is similar to (
4.49
), but we write down the argument again for safety.
Denote

\mathcal{F}:=\overrightarrow{h}^{X,*}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{g}}\otimes\IC_{V\boxtimes W}\langle-d_{V}-d_{W}\rangle\in\mathrm{Shv}(\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1,2\}}).

We can identify both maps in (
4.62
) as maps

(4.63)

\begin{split}\pi_{\{1,2\},!}\circ\mathrm{coev}_{\mathrm{g}}=\mathrm{coev}\circ\pi_{\{1\},!}:&\Hom(\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g},!}\Delta^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{g}}\mathcal{F},\Delta^{X,!}_{\mathrm{g}}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}})\\
\to&\Hom(\pi_{\{1,2\},!}\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g},!}\mathcal{F},\pi_{\{1,2\},!}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}})\end{split}

Consider commutative diagrams

(4.64)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\{1,2\},!}\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g},!}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\{1,2\},!}\Delta^{X}_{\mathrm{g},!}\Delta^{X,*}_{\mathrm{g}}\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g},!}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}\pi_{\{1\},!}\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g},!}\Delta^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{g}}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\{1,2\},!}\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g},!}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})^{*}\pi_{\{1,2\},!}\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g},!}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}\pi_{\{1\},!}\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g},!}\Delta^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{g}}\mathcal{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

and

(4.65)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}\pi_{\{1\},!}\Delta^{X,!}_{\mathrm{g}}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\{1,2\},!}\Delta^{X}_{\mathrm{g},!}\Delta^{X,!}_{\mathrm{g}}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\{1,2\},!}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}\pi_{\{1\},!}\Delta^{X,!}_{\mathrm{g}}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})^{!}\pi_{\{1,2\},!}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\{1,2\},!}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

Here the first arrow in the bottom of (
4.65
) uses the Beck-Chevalley base change map
\pi_{\{1\},!}\Delta^{X,!}_{\mathrm{g}}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}\to(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})^{!}\pi_{\{1,2\},!}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}
which can be identified with the natural purity isomorphism
\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C}\langle-2\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{P}_{X}\boxtimes\underline{k}_{C^{2}}
using
?THM?
.

Fix an element
\alpha\in\Hom(\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g},!}\Delta^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{g}}\mathcal{F},\Delta^{X,!}_{\mathrm{g}}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}})
. Unwinding definitions, one can see that the map
\pi_{\{1,2\},!}\circ\mathrm{coev}_{\mathrm{g}}(\alpha)
can be identified with the composition of the top row of (
4.64
) and
(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}\pi_{\{1\},!}(\alpha)
and the top row of (
4.65
). The map
\mathrm{coev}\circ\pi_{\{1\},!}(\alpha)
can be identified with the composition of the bottom row of (
4.64
) and
(\mathrm{id}\times\Delta_{C})_{!}\pi_{\{1\},!}(\alpha)
and the bottom row of (
4.65
). The commutativity of (
4.64
) follows from
?THM?
. The commutativity of (
4.65
) follows from a similar argument.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of
?THM?
assuming
?THM?
.

Keep our notation the same as the previous proof. The pull-back functoriality in
§4.2.3
gives us

(4.66)

f_{I,\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}^{*}:\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{sl}}\langle d_{I}\rangle)\to\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,I},\IC_{V^{I}}}(\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{g}},\delta^{X}_{I,\mathrm{g}}\langle d_{I}\rangle)

∎

for

.
Given the compatibility of the pull-back functoriality in
§4.2.3
and composition
§4.2.4
, we only need to show

(4.67)

\begin{split}f_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}^{*}\circ\mathrm{coev}_{\mathrm{sl}}=\mathrm{coev}_{\mathrm{g}}\circ f_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}^{*}:&\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,\{1\}},\IC_{V\otimes W}}(\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}-2\rangle)\\
\to&\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{g}}_{G,\{1,2\}},\IC_{V\boxtimes W}}(\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}},\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)\end{split}.

Denote

\mathcal{F}:=\overrightarrow{h}^{X,*}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{sl}}\otimes\IC_{V\boxtimes W}\langle-d_{V}-d_{W}\rangle\in\mathrm{Shv}(\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,\{1,2\}}).

Then the maps in (
4.67
) are identifies with maps

(4.68)

\begin{split}f_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}^{*}\circ\mathrm{coev}_{\mathrm{sl}}=\mathrm{coev}_{\mathrm{g}}\circ f_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}^{*}:&\Hom(\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl},!}\Delta^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{sl}}\mathcal{F},\Delta^{X,!}_{\mathrm{sl}}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}})\\
\to&\Hom(f^{*}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl},!}\mathcal{F},f^{*}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}})\end{split}.

Consider commutative diagrams

(4.69)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl},!}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}\Delta^{X}_{\mathrm{sl},!}\Delta^{X,*}_{\mathrm{sl}}\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl},!}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{X}_{\mathrm{g},!}f^{*}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl},!}\Delta^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{sl}}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl},!}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{X}_{\mathrm{g},!}\Delta^{X,*}_{\mathrm{g}}f^{*}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl},!}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{X}_{\mathrm{g},!}f^{*}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl},!}\Delta^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{sl}}\mathcal{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

and

(4.70)

\hbox to399.89pt{\vbox to62.73pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 199.94077pt\lower-31.36504pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

Here the first arrow in the top of (
4.70
) uses the Beck-Chevalley base change map
f^{*}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}\Delta^{X,!}_{\mathrm{sl}}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}\to\Delta^{X,!}_{\mathrm{g}}f^{*}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}\to\mathrm{sl}}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}
, which is identified with the natural purity isomorphism
\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{g}}\langle-2\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Delta^{X,!}_{\mathrm{g}}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{g}}
defined in
?THM?
.

?THM?
. The rest of the argument is similar to the previous proof, and we omit it.

4.6.4.
Proof of
?THM?
assuming
?THM?

Now we come to the technical heart of this section.

Proof of
?THM?
assuming
?THM?
.

Consider
U:=C^{2}\backslash\Delta_{C}(C)
.
Consider the diagram

(4.71)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(LX/L^{+}G)_{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(LX/L^{+}G)_{C^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(LX/L^{+}G)_{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{X}_{\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{C^{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

where both squares are Cartesian.
Consider the excision fiber sequence in
?THM?
:

(4.72)

j^{X}_{!}j^{X,*}\IC_{V\boxtimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ \delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}\to\IC_{V\boxtimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ \delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}\to\Delta^{X}_{\mathrm{sl},!}\Delta^{X,*}_{\mathrm{sl}}\IC_{V\boxtimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ \delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}.

It gives rise to the left column of the following diagram

(4.73)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom^{0}(\Delta^{X}_{\mathrm{sl},!}\Delta^{X,*}_{\mathrm{sl}}\IC_{V\boxtimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ \delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom^{0}(\IC_{V\otimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ \delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}-2\rangle)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom^{0}(\IC_{V\boxtimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ \delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom^{0}(\IC_{V\otimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ \delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}}\ \langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom^{0}(j^{X}_{!}j^{X,*}\IC_{V\boxtimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ \delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{adj}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gys}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot c_{1}(T_{C})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{X,*}_{\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{X,*}}

Here, the map
\mathrm{adj}
is induced by
(\Delta^{X}_{\mathrm{sl},!},\Delta^{X,!}_{\mathrm{sl}})
-adjunction and the isomorphism
\Delta^{X,!}_{\mathrm{sl}}\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}\langle-2\rangle
in
?THM?
. The map
\cdot c_{1}(T_{C})
is the cup product with the first Chern class of the tangent bundle
T_{C}
of the curve
C
. The map
\Delta^{X,*}_{\mathrm{sl}}
is induced by the pull-back functoriality of cohomological correspondence in
?THM?
.

Note that by definition of the map
\mathrm{coev}_{\mathrm{sl}}
given in (
4.56
) we have

\mathrm{coev}_{\mathrm{sl}}=\mathrm{Gys}\circ\mathrm{adj}^{-1}.

Therefore, to prove
?THM?
is to prove

(4.74)

c_{V\boxtimes W}^{\mathrm{sl}}-c_{W\boxtimes V}^{\mathrm{sl}}=\mathrm{Gys}\circ\mathrm{adj}^{-1}(c_{V\otimes W}^{\mathrm{sl}})

Lemma 4.56
.

The square in (
4.73
) is commutative.

Proof of
?THM?
.

Write
\mathcal{F}:=\IC_{V\boxtimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ \delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}\langle-d_{V}-d_{W}\rangle
. Recall
\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}=i_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl},*}\underline{k}_{(L^{+}X/L^{+}G)_{C^{2}}}

i_{|I|}=i_{I,\mathrm{sl}}:(L^{+}X/L^{+}G)_{C^{I}}\to(LX/L^{+}G)_{C^{I}}

for
I=\{1,2\},\{1\}
, write

\Delta=\Delta^{X}_{\mathrm{sl}}:(LX/L^{+}G)_{C}\to(LX/L^{+}G)_{C^{2}}

and
\underline{k}=\underline{k}_{(L^{+}X/L^{+}G)_{C^{2}}}
. Then we can rewrite the square in (

For any
\alpha\in\Hom^{0}(\Delta^{+,*}\mathcal{G},\Delta^{+,!}\underline{k})
, consider the following diagram

(4.77)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{+,*}\mathcal{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{+,*}\Delta^{+}_{*}\Delta^{+,*}\mathcal{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{+,!}\underline{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{+,*}\Delta^{+}_{*}\Delta^{+,!}\underline{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{*}\underline{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{+,*}\Delta^{+}_{*}\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where arrows without names are induced by natural adjunctions. It is easy to see
\Delta^{+,*}\circ\mathrm{Gys}\circ\mathrm{adj}^{-1}(\alpha)
gives the upper route from the left-top of the diagram (
4.77
) to the right-bottom of the diagram, while
\alpha\cdot c_{1}(T_{C})
gives the lower route. The commutativity of the square in (
4.77
) is tautological. This concludes the proof of
?THM?
.

∎

Consider the following diagram

(4.78)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom^{0}(\IC_{V\otimes W}*\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}-2\rangle)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom^{0}(\IC_{V\otimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ \delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}-2\rangle)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom^{0}(\IC_{V\otimes W}*\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom^{0}(\IC_{V\otimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ \delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot\hbar}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot c_{1}(T_{C})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}}

where the map
f^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}
is defined via the pull-back functoriality of cohomological correspondences in
§4.2.3
.

Lemma 4.57
.

The diagram (
4.78
) is commutative.

Proof of
?THM?
.

This follows from the fact that the composition
C\to B\Aut(D)\to B\mathbb{G}_{m}
is the map defining
T_{C}\in\Pic(C)
, hence gives us
\mathrm{Coor}^{*}\hbar=c_{1}(T_{C})
. See
§2.11
for our choice of
\hbar
.
∎

We make the following observation:

Lemma 4.58
.

We have

\Delta^{X,*}c_{V\boxtimes W}^{\mathrm{sl}}=f^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}c_{V\boxtimes W}^{\mathrm{l}}\in\Hom^{0}(\IC_{V\otimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.91995pt\Large$\star$}\ \delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}}\ \langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)

and

\Delta^{X,*}c_{W\boxtimes V}^{\mathrm{sl}}=f^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}c_{W\boxtimes V}^{\mathrm{l}}\in\Hom^{0}(\IC_{V\otimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.91995pt\Large$\star$}\ \delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}}\ \langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle),

where
\Delta^{X,*}:=\Delta^{X,*}_{\mathrm{sl}}
is the middle horizontal arrow in (
4.73
),
f^{\hk,*}_{\{1\},\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}
is the bottom arrow in (
4.78
), and
c_{V\boxtimes W}^{\mathrm{l}}
,
c_{W\boxtimes V}^{\mathrm{l}}
are defined in (
4.34
).

Proof.

This follows directly from the compatibility of pull-back and composition of cohomological correspondences in
§4.2.3
and
§4.2.4
.
∎

Lemma 4.59
.

We have

(4.79)

j^{X,*}c_{V\boxtimes W}^{\mathrm{sl}}=j^{X,*}c_{W\boxtimes V}^{\mathrm{sl}}\in\Hom^{0}(j^{X}_{!}j^{X,*}\IC_{V\boxtimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.91995pt\Large$\star$}\ \delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle).

Proof.

Define
\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,U}=l_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}^{\hk,-1}(U)
where
l_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}^{\hk}:\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,\{1,2\}}\to C^{2}
is the map remembering only legs. Consider the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(LX/L^{+}G)_{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{sl}}_{G,U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(LX/L^{+}G)_{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(LX/L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{l}}_{G})^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(LX/L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}^{\times 2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overrightarrow{h}^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{\hk}_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}})^{\times 2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}^{\times 2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\llcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overleftarrow{h}^{X}_{\mathrm{l}})^{\times 2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overrightarrow{h}^{X}_{\mathrm{l}})^{\times 2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

where both squares are Cartesian. Then we have

j^{X,*}c_{V\boxtimes W}^{\mathrm{sl}}=(f^{\hk}_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}})^{\times 2,*}(c_{V}^{\mathrm{l}}\boxtimes c_{W}^{\mathrm{l}})=j^{X,*}c_{W\boxtimes V}^{\mathrm{sl}}

and we are done.
∎

Denote
\epsilon=c_{V\boxtimes W}^{\mathrm{sl}}-c_{W\boxtimes V}^{\mathrm{sl}}-\mathrm{Gys}\circ\mathrm{adj}^{-1}(c_{V\otimes W}^{\mathrm{sl}})
. We have

(4.80)

\begin{split}\Delta^{X,*}\epsilon&=\Delta^{X,*}(c_{V\boxtimes W}^{\mathrm{sl}}-c_{W\boxtimes V}^{\mathrm{sl}})-\Delta^{X,*}\circ\mathrm{Gys}\circ\mathrm{adj}^{-1}(c_{V\otimes W}^{\mathrm{sl}})\\
&=f^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}(c_{V\boxtimes W}^{\mathrm{l}}-c_{W\boxtimes V}^{\mathrm{l}})-c_{V\otimes W}^{\mathrm{sl}}\cdot c_{1}(T_{C})\\
&=f^{\hk,*}_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}(c_{V\boxtimes W}^{\mathrm{l}}-c_{W\boxtimes V}^{\mathrm{l}}-c_{V\otimes W}^{\mathrm{l}}\cdot\hbar)\\
&=0\end{split},

where the second equality uses
?THM?
and
?THM?
, the third equality uses
?THM?
, and the last equality follows from our choice of
c_{V\otimes W}^{\mathrm{l}}
in
?THM?
(
2
)
.

On the other hand, we have

(4.81)

j^{X,*}\epsilon=j^{X,*}(c_{V\boxtimes W}^{\mathrm{sl}}-c_{W\boxtimes V}^{\mathrm{sl}})-j^{X,*}\circ\mathrm{Gys}\circ\mathrm{adj}^{-1}(c_{V\otimes W}^{\mathrm{sl}})=0-0=0

where the second identity uses
?THM?
and
j^{X,*}\circ\mathrm{Gys}=0
.

We can conclude our proof given the following lemma:

Lemma 4.60
.

Under
?THM?
, the map
\cdot c_{1}(T_{C})
in (
4.73
) is injective.

Assuming the lemma, we can conclude the proof of
?THM?
as follows. Since
j^{X,*}\epsilon=0
and the left column of (
4.73
) is exact, there exists
\epsilon_{0}\in\Hom^{0}(\Delta^{X}_{\mathrm{sl},!}\Delta^{X,*}_{\mathrm{sl}}\IC_{V\boxtimes W}\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\square$}\raisebox{0.49515pt}{\hskip 0.89996pt\Large$\star$}\ \delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}},\delta^{X}_{\{1,2\},\mathrm{sl}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}\rangle)
at the upper-left corner of (
4.73
) such that
\epsilon=\mathrm{Gys}(\epsilon_{0})
. By the commutativity of the diagram (
4.73
) we know
\mathrm{adj}(\epsilon_{0})\cdot c_{1}(T_{C})=\Delta^{X,*}\circ\mathrm{Gys}(\epsilon_{0})=\Delta^{X,*}\epsilon=0
. We can conclude
\epsilon_{0}=0
by
?THM?
and get
\epsilon=0
, which concludes the proof of
?THM?
.
∎

We denote
\mathcal{F}:=\IC_{V\otimes W}*\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle-d_{V}-d_{W}+2\rangle\in\mathrm{Shv}(LX/L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D))
, then the map we care about becomes

(4.83)

\cdot c_{1}(T_{C}):\Hom^{0}(f^{*}_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}\mathcal{F},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{sl}})\to\Hom^{0}(f^{*}_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}\mathcal{F},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{sl}}\langle 2\rangle).

We further denote
\mathcal{G}:=i_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}\mathcal{F}
and
\underline{k}=\underline{k}_{B\Aut(D)}
.
Note that

(4.84)

\begin{split}\Hom(f^{*}_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}\mathcal{F},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{sl}})&=\Hom(f^{*}_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}\mathcal{F},i_{\mathrm{sl},*}\underline{k}_{(L^{+}X/L^{+}G)_{C}}),\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Hom(i_{\mathrm{sl}}^{*}f^{*}_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}\mathcal{F},\underline{k}_{(L^{+}X/L^{+}G)_{C}}),\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Hom(f^{+,*}_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l}}\mathcal{G},\underline{k}_{(L^{+}X/L^{+}G)_{C}}),\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Hom(\mathcal{G},f^{+}_{\mathrm{sl}\to\mathrm{l},*}l_{\mathrm{sl}}^{*}\mathrm{Coor}^{*}\underline{k}),\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Hom(\mathcal{G},l_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}\mathrm{Coor}_{*}\mathrm{Coor}^{*}\underline{k}),\end{split}

where the last step uses
?THM?
(
2
)
, since
L^{+}X\rightarrow L^{+}X/L^{+}G\rtimes\mathrm{Aut}(D)
and
L^{+}X\rightarrow B\mathrm{Aut}(D)
are pro-smooth surjections.

Under the identification (
4.84
), the map (
4.83
) becomes

(4.85)

\cdot\hbar:\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},l_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}\mathrm{Coor}_{*}\mathrm{Coor}^{*}\underline{k})\to\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},l_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}\mathrm{Coor}_{*}\mathrm{Coor}^{*}\underline{k}\langle 2\rangle).

induced by the map
\hbar:\underline{k}\to\underline{k}\langle 2\rangle
.

Note the following fact:

Lemma 4.61
.

\mathrm{Coor}_{*}\mathrm{Coor}^{*}\underline{k}=\mathrm{cofib}(\underline{k}\langle-4\rangle\xrightarrow{\hbar^{2}}\underline{k})\oplus\mathrm{cofib}(\underline{k}\langle-2\rangle\xrightarrow{\hbar}\underline{k})^{\oplus 2g}[-1]
.

Proof of
?THM?
.

Note that we have a natural equivalence of categories

(4.86)

\Hom(\underline{k},\bullet):\mathrm{Shv}(B\Aut(D))^{>-\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Mod}(k[\hbar])^{>-\infty},

since
\Hom(\underline{k},\underline{k})=k[\hbar]\in\mathrm{Mod}(k[\hbar])^{>-\infty}
. Here for a stable
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}
with a t-structure, we write
\mathscr{C}^{>-\infty}
to denote the full-subcategory of
\mathscr{C}
consisting of objects bounded on the left. Moreover, given
?THM?
(
2
)
, by the formality of the cohomology of
C
, we have

\Hom(\underline{k},\mathrm{Coor}_{*}\mathrm{Coor}^{*}\underline{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}k[\hbar]/(\hbar^{2})\oplus k^{2g}[-1]\in\mathrm{Mod}(k[\hbar])^{>-\infty}.

Our desired result follows as

k[\hbar]/(\hbar^{2})\oplus k^{2g}[-1]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{cofib}(k[\hbar]\langle-4\rangle\xrightarrow{\cdot\hbar^{2}}k[\hbar])\oplus\mathrm{cofib}(k[\hbar]\langle-2\rangle\xrightarrow{\cdot\hbar}k[\hbar])^{\oplus 2g}[-1].

∎

Denote
\mathcal{A}=l_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}\mathrm{cofib}(\underline{k}\langle-4\rangle\xrightarrow{\hbar^{2}}\underline{k})
and
\mathcal{B}=l_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}\mathrm{cofib}(\underline{k}\langle-2\rangle\xrightarrow{\hbar}\underline{k})[-1]
. We need to show that both maps

(4.87)

\cdot\hbar:\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{A})\to\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{A}\langle 2\rangle)

(4.88)

\cdot\hbar:\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{B})\to\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{B}\langle 2\rangle)

are injective.
Denote
\underline{k}_{X}=l_{\mathrm{l}}^{*}\underline{k}
. Note that we have

\Hom(\mathcal{G},\underline{k}_{X})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Hom(\IC_{V\otimes W}*\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle d_{V}+d_{W}-2\rangle)=\Hom_{\check{G}}(V\otimes W\langle-d_{V}-d_{W}+2\rangle,\mathrm{PL}_{X,\hbar}).

?THM?
(
1
)
translates to

(4.89)

\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\underline{k}_{X}[d])=0

for
d=-1,-2,-3
.

?THM?
(
1
)
translates to the map

(4.90)

\cdot\hbar:\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\underline{k}_{X})\to\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\underline{k}_{X}[2])

is injective.
For (
4.87
), we have the following diagram

(4.91)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\underline{k}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\underline{k}_{X}[-3])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\underline{k}_{X}[-2])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\underline{k}_{X}[2])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{A}[2])}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}

where both rows are exact sequences. Note that (
4.89
) implies that
\alpha
is surjective and
\beta
is injective. Since
\gamma
is injective by (
4.90
), we know
\delta
is injective and hence prove injectivity of (
4.87
).

For (
4.88
), we have a short exact sequence

\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\underline{k}_{X}[-1])\to\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{B})\to\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\underline{k}_{X}[-2]).

Note that left and right terms are zero by (
4.89
), we know
\Hom^{0}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{B})=0
, which implies injectivity of (
4.88
). This concludes the proof of
?THM?
.

∎

Proof of
?THM?
.

One combines all the arguments in
§4.6
.
∎

5.
Automorphic Clifford relations

In this section, we restrict to a special case (minuscule and Poisson-pure case) such that the commutator relations (
?THM?
) on global special cohomological correspondences simplify to what we call the automorphic Clifford relations (
?THM?
).

•

In
§5.1
, we introduce a construction of local special cohomological correspondences via derived fundamental classes.

•

In
§5.2
, we induce the minuscule and Poisson-pure condition and state the main result
?THM?
.

•

In
§5.3
, we introduce a way to compute a scalar to be determined in
?THM?
.

5.1.
Cohomological correspondence via derived fundamental class

In this section, we study a particular construction of the local special cohomological correspondences
c^{\mathrm{l}}_{V}
in
?THM?
via derived fundamental classes. For notations on moduli spaces and morphisms between them, we refer to
§4.3
(see
§4.3.5
for a summary).

We by default consider only moduli spaces with one leg with index set
I=\{1\}
and drop the subscript
\{1\}
everywhere.

5.1.1.
Local special cohomological correspondence and relative affine Grassmanian

We first recall the geometric interpretation of the space of local special cohomological correspondences

\mathrm{Corr}_{\hk^{\mathring{X},\mathrm{l}}_{G},\IC_{V}}(\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}},\delta^{X}_{\mathrm{l}}\langle d\rangle)

introduced in
?THM?
using relative Grassmanian given in
[
4
, §8.2]
.

We use
\Gr^{X}_{G}
to denote the relative affine Grassmanian associated to the smooth affine
G
-variety
X
, which can be defined by defining the relative Hecke stack
\hk^{X,\mathrm{l}}_{G}:=\Gr^{X}_{G}/L^{+}G\rtimes\Aut(D)
via the Cartesian diagram:
